#### `NEW:skills/d-mode/playbooks/commit-and-push.md`

원본 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md:1-9`를 대체. `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/shipping.md:1-12`와 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/babysit.md:1-10`의 역할 중 남는 부분을 흡수. 실패 사례 F01~F16, 금지 N1~N5.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/d-mode/playbooks/commit-and-push.md -->
    ### Commit and push

    Invoked at the end of every other playbook that changes files.

    **You own what reaches `main`. Small ordered commits, a gate that passed on the exact commit you push, then a fast-forward push. Nothing else reaches the remote.**

    1. **Clean the diff.** Read `git diff` for the files you changed. Remove narrating comments, guards that no caller needs, dead compatibility paths, debug output, and edits unrelated to the goal. Apply the **unslop** skill to changed prose. Run the **no-comments** skill when the diff adds comments you did not review.
    2. **Guard what you stage.** Read `git status --porcelain` before any `git add`. Stage by path (`git add <files>`, never `-A`), so a file you did not change stays out. Never stage a secret (`.env`, `*.pfx`, `*.key`, `*.pem`, `credentials*`, a config file that holds a password), a vendor binary the project does not already track, or build output (`bin/`, `obj/`, `.venv/`, `target/`, `dist/`, `node_modules/`). When such a path shows as untracked, propose a `.gitignore` line for it and leave it unstaged.
    3. **Commit in small, ordered units.** Each commit builds on its own. Subject: `type(scope): subject`, naming a real symbol when one carries the change. Body: 20 lines at most, with `Why:`, `What changed:`, and `Verification:`, empty ones dropped, per the **technical-writing** skill. The `Verification:` line lists each gate command with its exit code, or `manual (<what was run>)`. Order the commits so the sequence proves itself (the **sequence-verifiable-units** principle skill).
    4. **Find the gate.** Use the first source that names one: the project's `CLAUDE.md`, its `verify-<app>` skill, then the bundled language skill for the files you changed (**csharp-best-practices**, **python-best-practices**, **rust-best-practices**, **typescript-best-practices**, **pml-best-practices**). A project with no test command still has a gate: the build, plus the real artifact run once. A project whose proof cannot run from a shell (PML inside E3D, a C# tool that needs a vendor install) declares a manual gate with the line `dstack-gate: manual` in its `CLAUDE.md`, optionally followed by what to run. With no build, no test, and no declared manual gate, the gate is missing.
    5. **Run the gate on the commit you will push.** Run its commands one at a time and stop at the first non-zero exit. Green means every command exited 0 at this `HEAD`. A manual gate is green only when the user confirmed in this session that the manual run passed on this change. Ask once, name what to run, and record `Verification: manual (<what was run>)`. An unattended run, or a worker that may not ask, treats an unconfirmed manual gate as red.
    6. **Check the reach of the diff.** Run `git diff --stat origin/main..HEAD`. When it changes files outside the goal you restated at the start, show the stat and ask once before pushing.
    7. **Sync with the remote.** Run `git fetch origin main`, then `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD`. Exit 0 means `main` has not moved: go to step 8. Exit 1 means it moved: run `git rebase origin/main`, then run step 5 again, because the base changed under the commits. Any other exit code is an error: stop and report it.
    8. **Push.** Run `git push origin HEAD:main`. Then run `git fetch origin main` and confirm that `git rev-parse HEAD` equals `git rev-parse origin/main`.

    The command list for steps 7 and 8. Run one line at a time in the session's shell and stop at the first non-zero exit, except where the note says an exit code is a branch:

    ```text
    git fetch origin main
    git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD     (exit 0: skip the next line. exit 1: run it. other: stop)
    git rebase origin/main                            (then run the gate again)
    git push origin HEAD:main
    git fetch origin main
    git rev-parse HEAD
    git rev-parse origin/main                         (must print the same SHA as the line above)
    ```

    **When a step fails**

    | ID | Case | What you see | What you do |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | F01 | The gate is red on your commits | A build or test command exits non-zero | Do not push. Fix forward in a new commit, or amend a commit that is not on `origin/main`. Run the whole gate again. |
    | F02 | The gate is red on the base too | The same command fails on `origin/main` in a scratch worktree (`git worktree add --detach <dir> origin/main`) | Report the base failure in one line. Push only when your commits fail no command that the base passes. Remove the scratch worktree. |
    | F03 | The gate is missing | No build, no test, and no `dstack-gate: manual` line | Stop with the commits local. Report `verification: none available` and ask before any push. |
    | F04 | The manual gate is not confirmed | `dstack-gate: manual` is declared and the user has not confirmed a run on this change | Ask once, naming what to run. Unattended: stop with the commits local and report `verification: manual run pending`. |
    | F05 | The push is rejected as non-fast-forward | `! [rejected]` with `fetch first` or `non-fast-forward` | Someone pushed first. Go back to step 7. After 3 rejected pushes in one run, stop and report. |
    | F06 | The rebase stops on a conflict | `CONFLICT`, and `git status` shows a rebase in progress | Resolve it only when both sides are in files you changed and the right result is clear. Otherwise `git rebase --abort`, which restores your commits, and report the two commits that collide. |
    | F07 | The tree is dirty with work that is not yours | `git status --porcelain` lists files you did not change | Leave them unstaged. A push needs no clean tree. If step 7 must rebase, use `git rebase --autostash origin/main`. If the stash does not reapply, stop. The work is safe in `git stash list`. Never drop it. |
    | F08 | You are on a worktree branch, not `main` | `git branch --show-current` prints another name | In the worktree, `git rebase main`. In the main checkout, `git merge --ff-only <branch>`. Then run steps 5 to 8 there. |
    | F09 | `HEAD` is detached | `git branch --show-current` prints nothing | `git switch -c d/<slug>` to keep the commits on a branch, then handle it as F08. |
    | F10 | A commit hook rejects the commit | `git commit` exits non-zero and prints the hook's output | Fix what the hook reports, stage the fix by path, and commit again. Never pass `--no-verify`. |
    | F11 | A push hook or the server refuses the push | `pre-push hook` output or `protected branch` | Report the exact message. Never pass `--no-verify`. Never change the remote's settings. |
    | F12 | Authentication fails | `Authentication failed`, `Permission denied`, or a credential prompt that cannot be answered | Report it. The commits stay local. Never change the credential helper or the remote URL. |
    | F13 | A file shows as fully changed with no real edit | `git diff --stat` lists every line, and `git diff -w --ignore-cr-at-eol` for that file is empty | Line endings flipped. Do not commit it. `git restore <file>` when you made no real edit to it. Report the file and the repository's `.gitattributes` or `core.autocrlf` setting. |
    | F14 | A path is too long | `Filename too long` during a checkout, rebase, or worktree add on Windows | Report it and name the fix for the user to run: `git config --global core.longpaths true`. Do not change global git settings yourself. |
    | F15 | There is no `origin` remote | `git remote` prints nothing | Commit only. Report that nothing was pushed. |
    | F16 | A pushed commit turns out wrong | A check or a user finds the break after step 8 | `git revert <sha>`, run the gate, and push the revert. Never reset or rewrite `origin/main`. |

    **Never**

    - N1. `git push --force` or `--force-with-lease` to `main`.
    - N2. A push with a red, missing, or unconfirmed gate, unless the user said so for this push in so many words.
    - N3. `git add -A`, `git stash drop`, or `git reset --hard` on work you did not create.
    - N4. `git commit --amend` or a rebase that rewrites a commit already on `origin/main`.
    - N5. `--no-verify` on a commit or a push.

    **Reply:** the commits pushed as `<old sha>..<new sha>`, each gate command with its exit code or the manual run the user confirmed, any failure case you hit by its ID and what you did, and anything left local.
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/d-mode/playbooks/autopilot.md`

원본 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autopilot-full.md:1-25`와 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autopilot-stack.md:1-8`을 하나로 합쳤다.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/d-mode/playbooks/autopilot.md -->
    ### Autopilot

    **You own the verdicts, never the changes. One owner carries each queue item from build to verified, and nothing lands on `main` until a verifier that never wrote the code has proven the code that lands.**

    A verdict covers one patch. A rebase that leaves `git patch-id` unchanged keeps the verdict, and any other change to the patch needs a fresh check.

    1. **The operator's gates come first.** The grant covers only the queue's items. Items the operator names as theirs stop at verified. A request to state the plan is not a go. "Don't push" or "I'll land them" means every item stops at verified, on its own branch, and nobody pushes.
    2. **Spawn one owner per item, each in its own worktree.** Each owner is a `dstack:d-agent` with `isolation: "worktree"` on a branch named `d/<item-slug>` off `main`. It builds the change, proves it on the real artifact, cleans the diff and commits per steps 1 to 3 of `playbooks/commit-and-push.md`, runs the gate, and reports its branch, head SHA, and `git patch-id`. Owners do not push and do not touch `main`.
    3. **Run owners in parallel off `main`.** Branch an item off another item's branch only when it builds on that item, and land the lower one first.
    4. **Verify each item with an agent that did not write it.** One verifier per item on the `swarm workers` model, per the **swarm** skill. It checks out the reported SHA, reruns the gate, drives the changed behavior the way a user would (the project's `verify-<app>` skill when one exists), audits the diff, and reports `PASS`, `PASS+NOTES`, or `FAIL` with the commands and their output. A green gate alone is not a verdict, because it does not run the change as a user would. Block an item only for a problem you can show: a failing run at its head, or a doc line the change made wrong. A problem `main` already has does not block unless the change copies it or newly reaches it.
    5. **Land verified items one at a time.** Landing is serial, because `main` is shared state (the **separate-before-serializing-shared-state** principle skill). For each item with `PASS` or `PASS+NOTES`, in queue order: in its worktree `git rebase main`, compare `git patch-id` before and after, and send the item back to step 4 when the patch changed. Then in the main checkout `git merge --ff-only d/<item-slug>` and run steps 5 to 8 of `playbooks/commit-and-push.md`. If its step 7 rebases again because `origin/main` moved, compare `git patch-id` once more before the push, and send the item back to step 4 when the patch changed. A `FAIL` item goes back to a fresh owner with the verifier's report, and the items behind it keep moving.
    6. **Keep the run honest.** Log each owner, verdict, and landing as a row per the **show-me-your-work** skill. On the operator's go for a long queue, arm Claude Code's built-in `/loop` at a one-hour interval to audit until no delegated work is left. An agent is stuck when it errors or shows nothing new past the time agents of its kind have taken. Replace a stuck agent with a fresh one that gets the original brief and the branch. Anything that loosens a gate, such as a skipped test or a raised budget, needs a verifier's proof and then your own countersign.
    7. **Stand down at once on the operator's stop.** A hold reaches every owner as a zero-writes order. Each reports where it stands and keeps its branch.

    **Reply:** catch the operator up per the **ketchup** skill: each item's state and verdict, what landed as `<old sha>..<new sha>`, countersigns and why, open operator gates, and parked follow-ups, with each branch name and the trail path.
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/d-mode/playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`

원본 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md:1-11`.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/d-mode/playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md -->
    ### Multi-phase plan

    **You own the plan, not the code. The plan is a checklist an owner runs box by box and the operator audits from the evidence.** The plan is the deliverable. Do not implement.

    1. When the change is one or two files with an obvious approach, skip the plan. Say so and stop.
    2. Settle open questions by prototype before you write, per `playbooks/prototype.md`. Ask the operator only about a product or preference call that no run can settle.
    3. Write the plan file. Open with what changes, the unit ids in landing order, and the dependency graph, which says which units are independent, which wait for which, and the files each may touch. Then one section per unit. One unit is one change with its own evidence, landed on `main` as one commit or a short run of ordered commits (the **sequence-verifiable-units** principle skill). Each section has checkboxes for what it depends on, the files it edits, the one change it builds, the result you see, and its verification.
    4. Tests alone are not sufficient verification. A unit is verified only when its unit, live, and perf boxes are all checked. Each live box drives the real surface with a concrete scenario and a pass predicate. The perf box names the metric, the probe, the `main` baseline measured first, and the number that fails. A unit that changes an interaction waits for the operator's review before it is pushed.
    5. Every unit leaves `main` green on its own, because each one lands through `playbooks/commit-and-push.md` before the next starts. A unit that cannot stand alone is merged into its neighbor in the plan.
    6. Hand back the plan path and stop. Execution starts on the operator's explicit go.

    **Reply:** the plan path, the unit ids with their dependencies and the review-gated set, and what stays unproven.
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/d-mode/playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`

원본 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md:1-12`.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/d-mode/playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md -->
    ### Worktree cleanup

    1. Snapshot and audit. Record free space with `Get-PSDrive -Name C` (use the drive that holds the repository), then run the audit (the **build-the-lever** principle skill):

       ```powershell
       powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "<this skill's folder>\scripts\worktree-audit.ps1" [-Base origin/<branch>] [-RepoPath <repo>]
       ```

       The same line works from Git Bash and from PowerShell. Without `-Base` it checks `MERGED` against `origin/HEAD`, then `origin/main`, then `origin/master`. It reads paths from `git worktree list`, never hand-typed, since a hand-typed path misses a worktree that lives under `.claude\worktrees` (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill). It classifies each worktree by size, age, merge state, uncommitted work, push state, and the newest session transcript that names it, then suggests a bucket: `hold-wip`, `verify-recent-chat`, `safe`, or `review`. The transcript scan is slow, so run it in the background, or pass `-SkipTranscripts` for a first look.
    2. The bucket is advice, not permission. The sessions still in use are the real artifact (the **prove-it-works** principle skill). Ask the user which sessions are still open and cross-check every candidate. The audit has marked `safe` a worktree whose session was still open, so the user's list wins.
    3. Verify usage before deleting. For every `verify-recent-chat` row, or anything you doubt, fan subagents out to read the transcripts and report whether the session is ongoing and which worktrees it touches (the **guard-the-context-window** principle skill, transcripts are bulk). A running session spawns arena and repro trees into sibling worktrees through background subagents, and those are in use even when the user never saw their names. Pick each subagent's reasoning level for the job, per the Subagents section.
    4. Pause on irreversible loss. `wip:N` is N tracked uncommitted edits. Show the diff and get a decision first, since removing a clean worktree is recoverable from its branch but uncommitted work is gone. `scratch:N` is untracked throwaway, safe to drop, but name the files. `REMOTE` of `no-remote` or `ahead<N>` with `MERGED` of `no` means commits that exist only on this machine. Removing the worktree keeps the branch, so keep the branch too unless the user says to delete it. Per Autonomy, clean and merged and not-in-use proceeds. `wip` and in-use pause.
    5. Prune the confirmed set. Per path, `git worktree remove --force <path>`. If the folder survives on ignored build output, `Remove-Item -LiteralPath <path> -Recurse -Force`, then `git worktree prune`. Branch refs survive, so no commits are lost. Confirm with `Get-PSDrive` and `git worktree list`.
    6. Other reclaimers, only when the user asks for more space. Build output (`bin`, `obj`, `target`, `dist`, `node_modules`) inside kept worktrees, `dotnet nuget locals all --clear`, `cargo cache` folders under `%USERPROFILE%\.cargo`, `pip cache purge`, and `%TEMP%`. Clear only caches the user has not said to keep.

    A worktree deep under a long path can fail on Windows with `Filename too long`. Report it and name `git config --global core.longpaths true` for the user to run.

    This is the one playbook that deletes user state with no code review to catch a slip, so the gates above are the review.

    **Reply:** free space before and after with the amount reclaimed, the worktrees pruned, and a one-line reason for each held back (in use by which session, uncommitted work, or unpushed commits).
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/d-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.ps1`

원본 `UP:skills/poteto-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.sh:1-134`의 PS 5.1 이식. PR 열을 빼고 버킷을 4종으로 줄였다. 추출 스크립트가 CRLF로 쓴다.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/d-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.ps1 -->
    #Requires -Version 5.1
    <#
    .SYNOPSIS
    Read-only worktree prune audit for Windows PowerShell 5.1.

    .DESCRIPTION
    Classifies every git worktree by size, age, merge state, uncommitted work,
    push state, and the newest Claude Code session transcript that names it.
    Prints a tab-separated table sorted by size with a suggested bucket.
    Never deletes anything. Deletion stays a human-gated step in the playbook.

    .PARAMETER Base
    Ref the MERGED column checks against, for example origin/integration.
    Default: origin/HEAD, then origin/main, then origin/master.

    .PARAMETER RepoPath
    Repository to audit. Default: the repository of the current directory.

    .PARAMETER RecentDays
    A transcript newer than this many days puts the worktree in verify-recent-chat.

    .PARAMETER TranscriptsRoot
    Folder that holds one transcript folder per workspace.
    Default: %USERPROFILE%\.claude\projects.

    .PARAMETER SkipTranscripts
    Skip the transcript scan. LAST_CHAT prints "-" for every row.

    .EXAMPLE
    powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File worktree-audit.ps1 -Base origin/main

    .NOTES
    Exit codes: 0 table printed, 1 not a repository or no base ref.
    #>
    [CmdletBinding()]
    param(
        [Alias('b')]
        [string]$Base = '',
        [Parameter(Position = 0)]
        [string]$RepoPath = '',
        [ValidateRange(0, 3650)]
        [int]$RecentDays = 4,
        [string]$TranscriptsRoot = '',
        [switch]$SkipTranscripts
    )

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'

    function Invoke-Git {
        param([string[]]$GitArgs)
        $previous = $ErrorActionPreference
        $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
        try {
            $output = & git @GitArgs 2>$null
            $code = $LASTEXITCODE
        }
        finally {
            $ErrorActionPreference = $previous
        }
        $lines = @()
        if ($null -ne $output) { $lines = @($output | ForEach-Object { [string]$_ }) }
        return New-Object psobject -Property @{ Code = $code; Lines = $lines }
    }

    function Get-FirstLine {
        param($Result)
        if ($Result.Code -ne 0 -or $Result.Lines.Count -eq 0) { return '' }
        return $Result.Lines[0].Trim()
    }

    function Write-Warn {
        param([string]$Message)
        [Console]::Error.WriteLine($Message)
    }

    function Stop-Audit {
        param([string]$Message)
        [Console]::Error.WriteLine($Message)
        exit 1
    }

    if ($RepoPath -eq '') {
        $RepoPath = Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('rev-parse', '--show-toplevel'))
        if ($RepoPath -eq '') { Stop-Audit 'not in a git repo; pass -RepoPath' }
    }
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $RepoPath -PathType Container)) { Stop-Audit "error: not a folder: $RepoPath" }
    Set-Location -LiteralPath $RepoPath
    if ((Invoke-Git @('rev-parse', '--git-dir')).Code -ne 0) { Stop-Audit "error: not a git repo: $RepoPath" }

    $worktrees = @()
    foreach ($line in (Invoke-Git @('worktree', 'list', '--porcelain')).Lines) {
        if ($line.StartsWith('worktree ')) { $worktrees += $line.Substring(9) }
    }
    if ($worktrees.Count -eq 0) { Stop-Audit 'error: git worktree list printed no worktrees' }
    $mainWorktree = $worktrees[0]

    if ($Base -eq '') {
        $candidates = @((Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('symbolic-ref', '--short', 'refs/remotes/origin/HEAD'))), 'origin/main', 'origin/master')
        foreach ($candidate in $candidates) {
            if ($candidate -eq '') { continue }
            if ((Invoke-Git @('rev-parse', '--verify', '--quiet', ($candidate + '^{commit}'))).Code -eq 0) {
                $Base = $candidate
                break
            }
        }
        if ($Base -eq '') { Stop-Audit 'error: no base ref (origin/HEAD, origin/main, origin/master all missing); pass -Base <ref>' }
        Write-Warn "warn: no -Base given; MERGED checks against $Base"
    }

    if ($Base.StartsWith('origin/')) {
        $branchName = $Base.Substring(7)
        $refspec = '+refs/heads/' + $branchName + ':refs/remotes/origin/' + $branchName
        if ((Invoke-Git @('fetch', 'origin', $refspec, '--quiet')).Code -ne 0) {
            Write-Warn "warn: could not fetch $Base; MERGED column may be stale"
        }
    }
    if ((Invoke-Git @('rev-parse', '--verify', '--quiet', ($Base + '^{commit}'))).Code -ne 0) { Stop-Audit "error: base ref not found: $Base" }

    $transcripts = ''
    if (-not $SkipTranscripts) {
        if ($TranscriptsRoot -eq '') {
            $userHome = $env:USERPROFILE
            if ([string]::IsNullOrEmpty($userHome)) { $userHome = $HOME }
            $TranscriptsRoot = Join-Path (Join-Path $userHome '.claude') 'projects'
        }
        $slug = [regex]::Replace($mainWorktree, '[^A-Za-z0-9]', '-')
        $candidateDir = Join-Path $TranscriptsRoot $slug
        if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $candidateDir -PathType Container) {
            $transcripts = $candidateDir
        }
        else {
            Write-Warn "warn: no transcript folder at $candidateDir; LAST_CHAT prints -"
        }
    }

    $epoch = New-Object DateTime 1970, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, ([DateTimeKind]::Utc)
    $now = [DateTime]::UtcNow
    $tab = [string][char]9
    $rows = @()

    foreach ($wt in $worktrees) {
        if ($wt -eq $mainWorktree) { continue }

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $wt -PathType Container)) {
            $missing = @('?', '?', 'no', 'missing', '-', '-', 'review', $wt) -join $tab
            $rows += New-Object psobject -Property @{ Bytes = [long]0; Line = $missing }
            continue
        }

        $measure = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $wt -Recurse -Force -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Measure-Object -Property Length -Sum
        $bytes = [long]0
        if ($null -ne $measure -and $null -ne $measure.Sum) { $bytes = [long]$measure.Sum }
        $sizeText = ($bytes / 1MB).ToString('0.0', [Globalization.CultureInfo]::InvariantCulture)

        $head = Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD'))
        $headStamp = Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'log', '-1', '--format=%ct', 'HEAD'))
        $age = '?'
        $seconds = [long]0
        if ([long]::TryParse($headStamp, [ref]$seconds) -and $seconds -gt 0) {
            $age = ([int][Math]::Floor(($now - $epoch.AddSeconds($seconds)).TotalDays)).ToString() + 'd'
        }

        $merged = 'no'
        if ($head -ne '' -and (Invoke-Git @('merge-base', '--is-ancestor', $head, $Base)).Code -eq 0) { $merged = 'YES' }

        $status = @((Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'status', '--porcelain')).Lines | Where-Object { $_ -ne '' })
        $tracked = @($status | Where-Object { -not $_.StartsWith('??') })
        $dirty = 'clean'
        if ($tracked.Count -gt 0) { $dirty = 'wip:' + $tracked.Count }
        elseif ($status.Count -gt 0) { $dirty = 'scratch:' + $status.Count }

        $branch = Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'symbolic-ref', '--quiet', '--short', 'HEAD'))
        $remote = 'detached'
        if ($branch -ne '') {
            $remote = 'no-remote'
            if ((Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'show-ref', '--verify', '--quiet', ('refs/remotes/origin/' + $branch))).Code -eq 0) {
                $remoteHead = Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'rev-parse', ('origin/' + $branch)))
                if ($remoteHead -eq $head) { $remote = 'pushed' }
                else { $remote = 'ahead' + (Get-FirstLine (Invoke-Git @('-C', $wt, 'rev-list', '--count', ('origin/' + $branch + '..HEAD')))) }
            }
        }

        $lastChat = '-'
        $recent = $false
        if ($transcripts -ne '') {
            $forward = $wt.Replace('\', '/')
            $escaped = $forward.Replace('/', '\\')
            $needles = @(($forward + '/'), ($forward + '"'), ($escaped + '\\'), ($escaped + '"'))
            $files = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $transcripts -Recurse -File -Filter '*.jsonl' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
            $newest = $null
            foreach ($file in $files) {
                if ($null -ne $newest -and $file.LastWriteTimeUtc -le $newest) { continue }
                $hit = Select-String -LiteralPath $file.FullName -SimpleMatch -Pattern $needles -List -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
                if ($null -ne $hit) { $newest = $file.LastWriteTimeUtc }
            }
            if ($null -ne $newest) {
                $lastChat = $newest.ToString('yyyy-MM-dd', [Globalization.CultureInfo]::InvariantCulture)
                if (($now - $newest).TotalDays -le $RecentDays) { $recent = $true }
            }
        }

        $bucket = 'review'
        if ($dirty.StartsWith('wip:')) { $bucket = 'hold-wip' }
        elseif ($recent) { $bucket = 'verify-recent-chat' }
        elseif ($merged -eq 'YES') { $bucket = 'safe' }

        $line = @($sizeText, $age, $merged, $dirty, $remote, $lastChat, $bucket, $wt) -join $tab
        $rows += New-Object psobject -Property @{ Bytes = $bytes; Line = $line }
    }

    Write-Output "# base: $Base"
    Write-Output (@('SIZE_MB', 'AGE', 'MERGED', 'DIRTY', 'REMOTE', 'LAST_CHAT', 'BUCKET', 'WORKTREE') -join $tab)
    foreach ($row in ($rows | Sort-Object -Property Bytes -Descending)) { Write-Output $row.Line }
    exit 0
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.ps1`

원본 `UP:skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh:1-42`의 PS 5.1 이식. 추출 스크립트가 CRLF로 쓴다.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.ps1 -->
    #Requires -Version 5.1
    <#
    .SYNOPSIS
    Append one well-formed row to a show-me-your-work decision log (TSV).

    .DESCRIPTION
    Stamps the UTC time, writes the header on first use, replaces tabs and line
    breaks inside a cell with spaces, and prefixes a cell whose first character a
    spreadsheet would read as a formula (= + - @) with a single quote. The log is
    read in spreadsheets, so text taken from commit subjects, file names, or
    generated output must not become a formula when a reviewer opens the file.
    Writes UTF-8 without a byte order mark and LF row endings.

    .EXAMPLE
    powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File log.ps1 -LogFile decisions.tsv -Phase harness -Decision 'froze the benchmark' -Why 'need one repeatable number' -Evidence 'commit 3a9f1c2' -Result 'baseline 412 ms'

    .NOTES
    Pass every cell as a named parameter. For a value that starts with "-", bind it
    with a colon: -Evidence:'-12% vs baseline'.
    Exit codes: 0 row appended, 1 the log could not be written.
    #>
    [CmdletBinding()]
    param(
        [Parameter(Mandatory = $true, Position = 0)]
        [ValidateNotNullOrEmpty()]
        [string]$LogFile,
        [Parameter(Mandatory = $true, Position = 1)]
        [AllowEmptyString()]
        [string]$Phase,
        [Parameter(Mandatory = $true, Position = 2)]
        [AllowEmptyString()]
        [string]$Decision,
        [Parameter(Mandatory = $true, Position = 3)]
        [AllowEmptyString()]
        [string]$Why,
        [Parameter(Mandatory = $true, Position = 4)]
        [AllowEmptyString()]
        [string]$Evidence,
        [Parameter(Mandatory = $true, Position = 5)]
        [AllowEmptyString()]
        [string]$Result
    )

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'

    function ConvertTo-Cell {
        param([AllowEmptyString()][string]$Value)
        $clean = [regex]::Replace($Value, '[\t\r\n]', ' ')
        if ($clean.Length -gt 0 -and '=+-@'.IndexOf($clean[0]) -ge 0) { $clean = "'" + $clean }
        return $clean
    }

    try {
        $path = $ExecutionContext.SessionState.Path.GetUnresolvedProviderPathFromPSPath($LogFile)
        $folder = [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName($path)
        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrEmpty($folder)) { [void][System.IO.Directory]::CreateDirectory($folder) }

        $utf8 = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
        $tab = [string][char]9
        $lf = [string][char]10
        $needsHeader = $true
        if ([System.IO.File]::Exists($path)) {
            $needsHeader = ((New-Object System.IO.FileInfo($path)).Length -eq 0)
        }
        if ($needsHeader) {
            [System.IO.File]::AppendAllText($path, ((@('ts', 'phase', 'decision', 'why', 'evidence', 'result') -join $tab) + $lf), $utf8)
        }

        $stamp = [DateTime]::UtcNow.ToString("yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss'Z'", [Globalization.CultureInfo]::InvariantCulture)
        $cells = @($stamp, (ConvertTo-Cell $Phase), (ConvertTo-Cell $Decision), (ConvertTo-Cell $Why), (ConvertTo-Cell $Evidence), (ConvertTo-Cell $Result))
        [System.IO.File]::AppendAllText($path, (($cells -join $tab) + $lf), $utf8)
    }
    catch {
        [Console]::Error.WriteLine('log.ps1: ' + $_.Exception.Message)
        exit 1
    }
    exit 0
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/setup-dstack/SKILL.md`

원본 `UP:skills/setup-pstack/SKILL.md:1-80`. 이전 기본값 이관 규칙(원본 20~28행)은 dstack에 이전 판이 없으므로 뺐다.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/setup-dstack/SKILL.md -->
    ---
    name: setup-dstack
    description: Configure which models dstack uses per role and at what reasoning budget. Detects your available models and writes a rule file that overrides the skill defaults. Use for /dstack:setup-dstack, /setup-dstack, "configure dstack models", "dstack budget", or changing dstack's model choices.
    ---

    # Setup dstack

    Write `~/.claude/rules/dstack-models.md` (`%USERPROFILE%\.claude\rules\dstack-models.md` on Windows), a rule file that Claude Code loads into every new session and that sets dstack's model per role.

    ## The file

    | Property | Rule |
    |---|---|
    | Location | User scope: `~/.claude/rules/dstack-models.md`. Project scope, optional and written by hand: `.claude/rules/dstack-models.md`, which overrides the user file line by line. |
    | Format | No frontmatter. `#` lines are comments. Every other line is `<role>: <value>`, or for a panel role `<role>: <value>, <value>, ...`, or the settings line `shell: <value>`. |
    | `shell` | `bash` or `powershell`. The shell the session runs commands in. d-mode writes commands for it. Claude Code on Windows runs its Bash tool through Git Bash, so `bash` is the usual value there. |
    | Missing `shell` line | The agent uses the shell tool the session offers and says which. |
    | Malformed `shell` value | Treated as missing, and the skill that read it says so once. |
    | Value | `<model> <effort>`, `inherit`, or `codex`. `<model>` is `opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, or `fable`. `<effort>` is `low`, `medium`, `high`, or `xhigh`. |
    | `inherit` | The role runs on the session model. The caller omits `model` and `effort`. |
    | `codex` | A seat on another vendor through `subagent_type: "codex:codex-rescue"`. Valid only in the three panel roles. It falls back to `opus high` when the codex plugin is not installed. |
    | Missing file | Every role uses its default from step 6. |
    | Missing line | That role uses its default. |
    | Unknown role | Ignored. This skill drops it on the next run and tells the user. |
    | Malformed value | The role uses its default, and the skill that read it says so once. |
    | No `# defaults: 1` line | The file is honored as written, and d-mode tells the user once per session to rerun this skill. |

    ## Steps

    ### 1. Detect available models

    The detected models are the `model` values that the `Agent` tool schema lists in this session, each with the `effort` values it lists. The `codex` seat is detected when the session's agent list has `codex:codex-rescue`. If you cannot detect any, ask the user which of `opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, and `fable` they can use. Never write a model you have not confirmed. `inherit` is always valid.

    ### 2. Detect the shell

    The shell is `bash` when the session offers a `Bash` tool, which on Windows is Git Bash. It is `powershell` when the session offers only a PowerShell tool. Confirm it with one command: `echo $0` prints a path that ends in `bash` under Git Bash, and PowerShell prints an empty line. Record the result for step 6.

    ### 3. Load current state

    The default role-to-model mapping is the shape shown in step 6. If the file already exists, read it and treat its `# budget` line and its role values as the current choices. Otherwise start from those defaults. Drop a line whose role is not in step 6, and drop a value that the File table calls malformed. Tell the user which lines you dropped.

    ### 4. Budget, map, and confirm

    **(a) Ask for a budget.** Use `AskUserQuestion`. In `-p` mode it is absent, so ask in plain text. Offer these three options with these exact labels, and name the current budget when the file records one. With no file, say that `large` matches the skill defaults.

    - `large — xhigh reasoning`
    - `medium — high reasoning`
    - `small — medium reasoning`

    **(b) Apply it.** Build the working table from the defaults, and on a re-run keep any role the user changed by model, list, or alias (`inherit`, `codex`). Each role has a target effort, which is its default's effort moved by the budget. `large` keeps it, `medium` lowers it one step, and `small` lowers it two steps, on the ladder `xhigh` > `high` > `medium` > `low`. No role runs above `xhigh`, and a target at the bottom of the ladder stays there. Set the effort of every `<model> <effort>` value in the role, panel entries included, to the role's target. If the model does not list that effort, use its highest listed effort at or below the target, else mark the role as needing a choice. `inherit` and `codex` do not change. So `medium` turns `opus xhigh` into `opus high` and `opus high` into `opus medium`, and `small` turns them into `opus medium` and `opus low`.

    **(c) Show the roles and confirm.** Show every role with its value, marking any model not in the detected set as needing a choice, and marking `codex` as "falls back to opus high" when the seat was not detected. Also list each line step 3 dropped. Ask whether to accept as-is or change specific roles, offering the detected models at `xhigh` or below plus `inherit`, and `codex` for panel roles. For panel roles (`arena runners`, `interrogate reviewers`) the value is a list, and one subagent runs per entry, so the list length sets the count. `arena cross-judge pool` is also a list, but Arena runs one judge. It takes the first entry from another vendor than the session model, so `codex` comes first when detected. `swarm workers` is the default model for every worker unless a race or comparison assigns another model per arm.

    ### 5. Validate

    Every `<model> <effort>` written must be in the detected set and at `xhigh` or below. `inherit` always passes. `codex` passes only in a panel role. If a chosen value fails, stop and ask again. Write nothing until every role passes.

    ### 6. Write the file

    Write the user-scope file with no frontmatter, the `# defaults: 1` line, a `# budget` line with the chosen label and the effort that label names, the `shell:` line from step 2, and one line per role, using the same labels d-mode uses. Create the `rules` folder when it is missing. Overwrite the whole file so re-runs stay idempotent. Never put the role lines in `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md`. Shape:

    ```
    # dstack model configuration. One line per role. Delete a line to fall back to the skill default.
    # Value: <model> <effort> | inherit | codex. model: opus, sonnet, haiku, fable. effort: low, medium, high, xhigh.
    # inherit: the role runs on the session model. codex: a codex:codex-rescue seat, panel roles only.
    # shell: bash | powershell. The shell this machine's sessions run commands in.
    # defaults: 1
    # budget: large (xhigh)
    shell: bash
    feature, refactoring: opus xhigh
    bug-fix: opus xhigh
    perf-issue: opus xhigh
    hillclimb: opus xhigh
    judgment and prose: opus xhigh
    arena runners: fable high, opus high, codex
    arena cross-judge pool: codex, opus high
    swarm workers: opus high
    interrogate reviewers: fable high, opus high, codex
    ```

    ### 7. Confirm

    Read the file back and show it. Tell the user it applies to new sessions. Re-running this skill updates it.

    ### 8. Offer a verification skill (optional)

    Check whether the project has a way to drive the real app for proof (a `verify-*` skill under `.claude/skills/`, or an existing harness). If not, offer once: "want a project-local verification skill, so agents can drive the app the way a user does and prove changes work? I can generate one with /dstack:create-verification-skill." On yes, `Read` `../create-verification-skill/SKILL.md` and follow it. On no, move on without pushing.

    ### 9. Offer a formal verification skill (optional)

    Check whether the project already has a formal verification skill (a project skill that runs a model checker, a proof, or another verifier against the code). If not, offer once: "want a formal verification skill, so agents can prove a key property of your code holds in every case in scope, not only the cases tests reach? I can generate one with /dstack:create-formal-verification-skill." On yes, `Read` `../create-formal-verification-skill/SKILL.md` and follow it. On no, move on without pushing.
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/d-help/SKILL.md`

원본 `UP:skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md:1-163`.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/d-help/SKILL.md -->
    ---
    name: d-help
    description: Guides users through dstack setup, /dstack:d-mode, and picking the skill, playbook, or principle for a task. Type /dstack:d-help with a question.
    disable-model-invocation: true
    ---

    # D help

    Answer the user's question about dstack, hand them a prompt they can send, and link the file the answer came from. For a help question, don't start the work. The user asked how, and a dstack run spends real tokens, so let them send the prompt.

    A message that asks for work, such as "use dstack to fix this bug", is not a help question. Read [`d-mode`](../d-mode/SKILL.md), do the work under it, and mention once that typing `/dstack:d-mode` at the start of a task loads it.

    This file maps questions to the skills and guide pages that hold the answers. Those files own the details. Read the file you route to before you quote it, and trust it when it disagrees with this map. The links here point into the installed plugin, which the user may not be able to open, so give the user the file's public copy: `https://github.com/Donghun1q2w/d-stack/blob/main/` followed by its path. The guide pages and the README are written in Korean. Answer in the user's language.

    ## Find out what they need

    Infer the need from the message and the conversation. A named situation, such as "which skill reviews a branch?", goes straight to its section. If the need is still unclear, ask one multiple-choice question with `AskUserQuestion` and these options, then answer only the section they pick:

    - Get set up
    - Start a task with `/dstack:d-mode`
    - Pick a skill for a situation
    - Fix a run that went wrong
    - Make dstack my own

    Check the state that changes the answer, and mention it only when it does:

    - No `~/.claude/rules/dstack-models.md` means `/dstack:setup-dstack` hasn't run for this user, so every role uses its default model.
    - No `verify-*` skill or other app harness in the project means agents have no scripted way to drive the app. Mention `/dstack:create-verification-skill` when the question is about proving a change works.
    - No formal verification skill in the project means no property is checked past what tests reach. Mention `/dstack:create-formal-verification-skill` when the question is about a race, an invariant, or another case tests rarely hit. When one exists and the code it covers changed after the commit it records as last in sync, mention `/dstack:maintain-formal-verification-skill` if the question touches that code.

    When the model file is missing and it matters, ask whether the user wants to pick a model for each role and a reasoning budget now. It matters when the user is new, the question is about setup or cost, or the answer depends on which models run. Ask at most once per chat. If the need is also unclear, ask both questions together. Offer two choices:

    - Now: give them `/dstack:setup-dstack` to type, and answer their question too.
    - Later: answer their question, and add one line saying every role keeps its default model until they run `/dstack:setup-dstack`.

    ## Get set up

    1. Install with `/plugin marketplace add Donghun1q2w/d-stack`, then `/plugin install dstack@d-stack`.
    2. Run [`/dstack:setup-dstack`](../setup-dstack/SKILL.md). It asks for a reasoning budget, maps a model to each role, and writes `~/.claude/rules/dstack-models.md`. The file applies to new sessions. Then it offers once each to generate a verification skill and a formal verification skill for the project, unless the project already has one.
    3. Start a real task with `/dstack:d-mode`, a goal, and a check that can pass or fail.

    Installing changes little until the user invokes a skill. `/dstack:setup-dstack` loads from the user's words, and the four language skills load when a session opens a file their `paths` match. Every other skill waits for its slash command. The [README](../../README.md) and [guide page 1](../../docs/guide/01-setup.md) have the details. Offer to word their first prompt with them, per [`references/prompting.md`](references/prompting.md).

    If cost is the worry, say where the tokens go and how to spend fewer. dstack spends extra tokens on subagents and review panels. Rerun `/dstack:setup-dstack` and pick a smaller budget or cheaper models. A role set to `inherit` runs on the session's model. A shorter panel list runs fewer subagents, one for each entry. Save `/dstack:d-mode` for work that needs rigor.

    dstack is built for Claude Code on Windows. Its two helper scripts are Windows PowerShell 5.1, and its gates are plain command lists that run in Git Bash or PowerShell. The panel's third seat uses the codex plugin's `codex:codex-rescue` agent when it is installed and falls back to an Opus seat when it is not.

    ## Start a task with `/dstack:d-mode`

    `/dstack:d-mode` matches the task to a playbook, copies the playbook's steps into the todo list, and runs the other skills as the steps need them. A good prompt states the goal and how to tell it's done. It doesn't list skills, because a hand-written sequence tends to drop or reorder steps the playbook would keep. Read [`references/prompting.md`](references/prompting.md) before you help word one. [Guide page 2](../../docs/guide/02-d-mode.md) has examples.

    The skill stays in the conversation after the user types `/dstack:d-mode` once. After a `/clear`, a compaction that drops it, or in a new session, they type it again. Mid-chat, "new task" makes the mode match a fresh playbook. `/dstack:d-mode` already uses `dstack:d-agent` for the subagents its playbook steps spawn. To get the same style from a subagent of your own, spawn it with `subagent_type: "dstack:d-agent"`.

    ## Pick a skill

    The default answer is `/dstack:d-mode`, which runs most of the others when its steps need them. Name a skill directly when the user wants more or less of something than the playbook gives. Read the skill before you recommend it, and give one example prompt. Every command below takes the `/dstack:` prefix, such as `/dstack:how`.

    | The user wants to | Skill |
    |---|---|
    | Do any non-trivial task with rigor | [`d-mode`](../d-mode/SKILL.md) |
    | Check that the agent understood their goal and the problem before it starts | [`in-your-own-words`](../in-your-own-words/SKILL.md) |
    | Get a prompt rewritten deeper, not bigger, before sending it | [`sharpen`](../sharpen/SKILL.md) |
    | Learn the expert words for what they're building and get their description rewritten with them | [`reframe`](../reframe/SKILL.md) |
    | Know how code works now, or where new code should live | [`how`](../how/SKILL.md) |
    | Know why code is shaped this way, or where a number came from | [`why`](../why/SKILL.md) |
    | Understand a change or subsystem, explained plainly | [`teach`](../teach/SKILL.md) |
    | Catch up on their own recent work on a topic | [`recall`](../recall/SKILL.md) |
    | Catch up on what changed in this chat since their last message | [`ketchup`](../ketchup/SKILL.md) |
    | Catch up like `ketchup`, in a short subtitled video of slides | [`ketchup-video`](../ketchup-video/SKILL.md) |
    | Know what a small diff could break outside itself | [`blast-radius`](../blast-radius/SKILL.md) |
    | Settle types and module shape before code that crosses a function boundary | [`architect`](../architect/SKILL.md) |
    | Get several attempts at one brief, merged into the best one | [`arena`](../arena/SKILL.md) |
    | Run parallel checks over slices, or race workers, each in its own worktree | [`swarm`](../swarm/SKILL.md) |
    | Have different models review a diff and try to break it | [`interrogate`](../interrogate/SKILL.md) |
    | Fix a bug test-first when a cheap local test exists | [`tdd`](../tdd/SKILL.md) |
    | Apply TypeScript rules to `.ts` or `.tsx` work | [`typescript-best-practices`](../typescript-best-practices/SKILL.md) |
    | Apply C# and .NET rules, with `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` | [`csharp-best-practices`](../csharp-best-practices/SKILL.md) |
    | Apply Python rules, with `pytest` | [`python-best-practices`](../python-best-practices/SKILL.md) |
    | Apply Rust rules, with `cargo test` and `cargo clippy` | [`rust-best-practices`](../rust-best-practices/SKILL.md) |
    | Apply AVEVA PML rules for E3D macros, forms, and objects | [`pml-best-practices`](../pml-best-practices/SKILL.md) |
    | Strip comments before review, using a reviewer that didn't write them | [`no-comments`](../no-comments/SKILL.md) |
    | Clean AI tells out of prose | [`unslop`](../unslop/SKILL.md) |
    | Write docs, an RFC, a README, or a commit message to a standard | [`technical-writing`](../technical-writing/SKILL.md) |
    | Hear the last reply again in plain words | [`bro`](../bro/SKILL.md) |
    | Give agents a scripted way to drive the app and prove behavior | [`create-verification-skill`](../create-verification-skill/SKILL.md) |
    | Bring a verification skill and its feature map back in line with the app | [`maintain-verification-skill`](../maintain-verification-skill/SKILL.md) |
    | Formally verify a race, invariant, or other property that tests rarely reach | [`create-formal-verification-skill`](../create-formal-verification-skill/SKILL.md) |
    | Bring a formal verification back in line after the code it covers changed | [`maintain-formal-verification-skill`](../maintain-formal-verification-skill/SKILL.md) |
    | Show a planner or designer what the user sees as a chart checked by a TLA+ model | [`statechart`](../statechart/SKILL.md) |
    | Vet a performance number before reporting or acting on it | [`benchmark-checklist`](../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) |
    | Run a large or cross-cutting change, or one to review after stepping away | [`figure-it-out`](../figure-it-out/SKILL.md) |
    | Keep a decision log during a run, and review it afterward | [`show-me-your-work`](../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md) |
    | Pick a model for each role and a reasoning budget | [`setup-dstack`](../setup-dstack/SKILL.md) |
    | Turn their own working habits into a personal mode skill | [`automate-me`](../automate-me/SKILL.md) |
    | Turn what a finished task taught into skill edits | [`reflect`](../reflect/SKILL.md) |
    | Stop agents from repeating the same mistakes in this repo | [`correct`](../correct/SKILL.md) |
    | Find their way around dstack | `d-help` |

    If a skill directory next to this one is missing from the table, read its frontmatter and route by its description. The `principle-*` directories are covered under principles below.

    Close calls:

    - `how` explains what the code does. `why` explains the reasons. `teach` runs one or both and explains the result plainly.
    - `arena` gives every worker the same brief and merges the best parts. `swarm` splits work into slices or a race and returns one report.
    - `interrogate` reviews the diff. `blast-radius` looks for breakage outside the diff and proves the one fact that makes the change safe.
    - `recall` rebuilds context across recent chats. `ketchup` covers only this chat since the user's last message. Resuming one specific chat or branch is the Session pickup playbook.
    - `figure-it-out` designs one rigorous run. The Autonomous run playbook drives one task to a finish condition.

    Not in dstack:

    - `/loop` is a Claude Code built-in.
    - The `codex:codex-rescue` agent ships in the codex plugin. dstack works without it.
    - dstack has no planning skill. Claude Code's plan mode works alongside it.

    ## Playbooks and principles

    Playbooks are step lists inside `/dstack:d-mode`, not skills, so they have no slash command. Inside `/dstack:d-mode`, describing the task picks one, and these phrases name one directly:

    - "commit and push" or "land this" runs Commit and push. It runs the project's gate, then pushes to `main` only when it passes.
    - "take over this branch" runs Session pickup.
    - "pause safely" runs Pause safely.
    - "full autopilot on this queue" runs Autopilot. "autopilot, don't push" stops each item at verified.
    - "run the eval playbook" runs Eval.

    The Playbooks section of [`d-mode`](../d-mode/SKILL.md) lists every playbook. [Guide page 6](../../docs/guide/06-verify-and-ship.md) covers verifying a change and pushing it.

    For work that spans phases, asking `/dstack:d-mode` for a plan runs the [Multi-phase plan playbook](../d-mode/playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md), which writes the plan and doesn't implement it. For a design question, the Prototype playbook or `architect` settles it in code first.

    Principles are one-rule skills that `/dstack:d-mode` reads and cites in its replies. The user rarely invokes one. They steer with the names instead, as in "apply prove it works. show me the real output." Typing `/dstack:principle-<name>` still loads one on demand. [Guide page 8](../../docs/guide/08-principles.md) lists them.

    ## Fix a run that went wrong

    | Symptom | Fix |
    |---|---|
    | The mode stopped applying | The context was cleared or compacted. Type `/dstack:d-mode` again at the start of the task. |
    | A question got treated as the next step of the last task | Say "new task", or say the turn doesn't need the mode. |
    | A new model choice had no effect | The file from `/dstack:setup-dstack` applies to new sessions. Start one. |
    | Runs cost more than expected | See the cost paragraph under Get set up. |
    | A skill didn't load on its own | Only `/dstack:setup-dstack` and the four language skills load on their own. The others load when the user types them or when `/dstack:d-mode` reads them, and it doesn't read every skill. |
    | Parallel agents overwrote each other | Give each agent its own worktree. `/dstack:d-mode` passes `isolation: "worktree"` when two agents could write the same checkout. |
    | An overnight run moved but finished nothing | `/loop` needs a check that can pass or fail, not a duration. See [guide page 7](../../docs/guide/07-overnight.md). |
    | The reply claims success from a green build | Ask for the real command, flow, stored value, or profile. That's the prove-it-works principle. |
    | A push was rejected | Someone pushed first. The Commit and push playbook fetches, rebases, reruns the gate, and pushes again. It never force-pushes. |
    | A PowerShell command failed on `&&` | Windows PowerShell 5.1 has no `&&`. Rerun `/dstack:setup-dstack` so the `shell:` line matches the session, and ask the agent to run the commands one at a time. |
    | Every push stops to ask about verification | The project has no build or test command. Add `dstack-gate: manual` to its `CLAUDE.md` with what to run, and confirm the manual run when asked. |

    For a run that drifts, [`references/prompting.md`](references/prompting.md) has one-line steers. [Guide page 10](../../docs/guide/10-recipes-and-pitfalls.md) has more pitfalls and the recipes worth copying.

    ## Make dstack my own

    - [`automate-me`](../automate-me/SKILL.md) drafts a personal mode skill from the user's own history, to use alongside `/dstack:d-mode`.
    - [`reflect`](../reflect/SKILL.md) after a session turns its lessons into skill edits the user approves.
    - `/dstack:d-mode write a skill for <workflow>` runs the authoring playbook. The eval playbook tests a skill change blind.
    - Fix a misbehaving skill in its own commit, not inside the feature work where it went wrong.
    - [`docs/EXTENDING.md`](../../docs/EXTENDING.md) has the naming contract and the steps for adding a language skill or a playbook.

    [Guide page 9](../../docs/guide/09-make-it-yours.md) covers each of these.

    ## Reply

    Lead with the answer. Give at most one example prompt in a code block, adapted from [`references/recipes.md`](references/recipes.md) when one fits, then the link to that file. Keep it short unless the user asked for the whole map.

#### `NEW:skills/csharp-best-practices/SKILL.md`

형식은 `UP:skills/typescript-best-practices/SKILL.md:1-31`. 조사 순서 규칙은 사용자의 csharp 스킬에서 가져왔다.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/csharp-best-practices/SKILL.md -->
    ---
    name: csharp-best-practices
    description: C# and .NET best practices with the dotnet build and test commands. Use when reading or editing any .cs, .csproj, .sln, or .xaml file.
    paths: ["**/*.cs", "**/*.csproj", "**/*.sln", "**/*.xaml"]
    ---

    # C# best practices

    Apply the **type-system-discipline** principle skill first.

    ## Build and test

    Work from the folder that holds the `.sln`. Run the commands one at a time from the folder named above and stop at the first non-zero exit. They are the gate for the Commit and push playbook. A project's own `CLAUDE.md` wins when it names different commands.

    ```text
    dotnet build -c Release
    dotnet test -c Release --no-build
    ```

    Add a line only when the project is set up for it:

    | Extra command | Add it when |
    |---|---|
    | `-warnaserror` on the build line | A project file sets `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, or the project's `CLAUDE.md` asks for it. |
    | `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` | The repository has an `.editorconfig`. |
    | `dotnet test` | The solution has a test project. Leave the line out when it has none. |

    | Project kind | Change to the gate |
    |---|---|
    | SDK-style project | The commands above. |
    | Legacy .NET Framework `.csproj` (WinForms or WPF with `packages.config`) | `dotnet build` can fail on it. Use `msbuild <solution>.sln /p:Configuration=Release` with the project's `Platform` from a Visual Studio developer shell, and `vstest.console.exe` for the tests. |
    | A project that references vendor DLLs outside the repo (AVEVA and similar) | The build needs the vendor install on the machine. Where it cannot run, the project declares `dstack-gate: manual` in its `CLAUDE.md`, and the gate is the user's confirmed run on a machine that has the install. Never mark an unrun build green. |
    | No test project | The gate is the build plus one run of the real exe. Say so. |

    ## Rules

    | Rule | Summary |
    |------|---------|
    | Research order | For an API you do not know cold, read Microsoft Learn first, then StackOverflow answers that target the project's .NET version, then real usage on GitHub. Cite the source in the reply. |
    | Target framework first | Read `<TargetFramework>` and `<PlatformTarget>` before writing code. Language features and APIs differ between .NET Framework 4.x and .NET 8, and an x86 vendor DLL forces x86. |
    | Nullable reference types | Keep `<Nullable>enable</Nullable>` on in SDK-style projects. Fix a warning by changing the type, not with `!`. |
    | Closed variants | Model a closed set as an abstract `record` with a private constructor and nested sealed cases. Match it with a `switch` expression whose discard arm throws, because the compiler cannot prove the set is closed, and cover every case in one test. Records need C# 9, so on older targets use a sealed class hierarchy. |
    | Strong ids | Wrap an id or unit in a `readonly record struct` so two ids cannot be swapped. Parse it once at the boundary. |
    | Immutable by default | `record`, `init` setters, and `IReadOnlyList<T>` for data that crosses a method boundary. |
    | Parse at the boundary | Turn config, files, CLI args, and network payloads into a typed model once. Inside, trust the type. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill. |
    | Exceptions | Throw for a broken invariant. Return `bool TryX(out T)` or a result type for an expected failure. Catch only what you can handle, and never `catch (Exception) { }`. |
    | `IDisposable` | `using` declarations for every disposable. A class that owns one implements `IDisposable`. |
    | Async | `async` all the way. No `.Result`, `.Wait()`, or `async void` outside an event handler. Pass `CancellationToken` through. |
    | UI thread | WinForms and WPF controls are touched only on the UI thread. Long work runs in `Task.Run` and returns through `await`, `IProgress<T>`, or the dispatcher. |
    | Logic out of code-behind | A form or window wires events and binds data. Rules live in plain classes a test can construct without a window. |
    | Vendor assemblies | Load DLLs that live in a vendor install folder through one `AssemblyResolve` handler registered before the first vendor type is touched, and keep the install path in configuration, never in code. |
    | Real tests | Test the public behavior with xUnit, NUnit, or MSTest, whichever the solution already uses. Don't mock what you can construct. |
    | Structured logging | `ILogger` message templates with named fields. No `Console.WriteLine` in shipped library code. |

    Examples: `references/patterns.md`.
<!-- END FILE -->

#### `NEW:skills/csharp-best-practices/references/patterns.md`

형식은 `UP:skills/typescript-best-practices/references/patterns.md`.

<!-- BEGIN FILE: skills/csharp-best-practices/references/patterns.md -->
    # C# patterns

    Code examples for the rules in `SKILL.md`. The underlying principles are language-agnostic. See the **type-system-discipline** and **boundary-discipline** principle skills.

    ## Closed variants

    ```csharp
    public abstract record LoadState
    {
        private LoadState() { }
        public sealed record Loading : LoadState;
        public sealed record Ready(Drawing Drawing) : LoadState;
        public sealed record Failed(string Error) : LoadState;
    }

    static string Describe(LoadState state) => state switch
    {
        LoadState.Loading => "loading",
        LoadState.Ready ready => ready.Drawing.Title,
        LoadState.Failed failed => failed.Error,
        _ => throw new InvalidOperationException($"unhandled state {state.GetType().Name}"),
    };
    ```

    The private constructor closes the set, since only nested types can derive from it. The compiler does not know that, so without the discard arm it reports CS8509 even when every case is listed, and `-warnaserror` fails the build. The throwing arm keeps the build green and turns a forgotten case into a loud failure that one test over all cases catches.

    ## Strong ids

    ```csharp
    public readonly record struct ElementId(string Value)
    {
        public static bool TryParse(string? input, out ElementId id)
        {
            id = default;
            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(input) || !input.StartsWith('/')) return false;
            id = new ElementId(input);
            return true;
        }
    }
    ```

    ## Expected failure without exceptions

    ```csharp
    public static bool TryReadSettings(string path, out Settings settings, out string error)
    {
        settings = default!;
        error = "";
        if (!File.Exists(path)) { error = $"settings file not found: {path}"; return false; }
        var parsed = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<Settings>(File.ReadAllText(path));
        if (parsed is null) { error = "settings file is empty"; return false; }
        settings = parsed;
        return true;
    }
    ```

    ## UI thread

    ```csharp
    private async void exportButton_Click(object sender, EventArgs e)
    {
        exportButton.Enabled = false;
        try
        {
            var report = await Task.Run(() => exporter.Export(selection, cancellation.Token));
            statusLabel.Text = $"{report.Rows} rows exported";
        }
        finally
        {
            exportButton.Enabled = true;
        }
    }
    ```

    An event handler is the one place `async void` is correct. The `await` returns to the UI thread, so the label update is safe.

    ## Vendor assemblies

    ```csharp
    static Assembly? ResolveVendor(object? sender, ResolveEventArgs args)
    {
        var name = new AssemblyName(args.Name).Name + ".dll";
        var path = Path.Combine(VendorSettings.InstallFolder, name);
        return File.Exists(path) ? Assembly.LoadFrom(path) : null;
    }

    static void Main()
    {
        AppDomain.CurrentDomain.AssemblyResolve += ResolveVendor;
        Run();
    }
    ```

    `VendorSettings.InstallFolder` comes from configuration. Keep every vendor type out of `Main` itself, because the runtime resolves a method's types before its first line runs.
<!-- END FILE -->
